## Current boundary - USF-1192 transition frontier closed, 2026-09-13

### State

- `c253cdd` on `anqr-experience-realisation`, clean tree, pushed, zero ahead. Gate 17 passed at `d41b0d1`: 1100 tests, 0 failures, exit 0, 67.1 min.
- `transition inspect --root urn:usf:work:cli-transition-definition --manifest graph/manifests/cli-transition-definition.json` returns `complete: true`, `TRANSITION_COVERAGE_COMPLETE`, **0 diagnostics** (was 25), 5 waves, 10 descriptors.
- `executionAuthority: none` and `parallelSafety: unproven` are unchanged and were not altered to reach that state. The graph requires an operator decision for the first; the second is deliberately fail-closed because all scopes are rule-derived, so no authored path list can prove the cohort. Never clear it from topology or empty conflicts.
- Hermes is live, cycle 175+, on `graph/manifests/anqr-application.json`, `ready: 0`, `lastReason: "no ready implementation work remains"`, zero tokens and zero provider contacts. It is not stuck; it has nothing to pick up because USF-1189's lane reports `boundLaneCount: 0` against six unbound issue locators.

### What closed the frontier

The 25 diagnostics were one cause, not twenty-five. `CLI_OPERATIONAL_RULE_UNRESOLVED` is suppressed only when `allDescriptorsValid` holds and every field user has a supporter, and operational support is computed only for descriptors whose projection is valid. Five stale `INVALID_REALISATION_BASIS` descriptors made `allDescriptorsValid` false, withholding the 13 rule diagnostics and 6 unauthenticated evaluators. One construction-authority republication against the current basis cleared all 25 with **no source change**.

Order held: gate green -> freeze source -> evidence -> prove -> verify -> apply under a steward claim, released immediately. Publishing and then touching source has staled authority three times in this programme.

### Accepted work

- Command registry 73 -> 103 rows. Every advertised leaf declares its own output schema, exit codes, contact posture and authority effect. Three group answers were wrong rather than coarse: `graph` declared `externalContact: explicit` so `graph integrity` appeared to contact a provider, `linear-binding` declared a schema no handler emits, and `providers` promised no semantic authority while `providers policy` writes a graph line through the ordinary transaction. Guarded by `tests/unit/commands/registry-leaf-coverage.test.ts`, which derives the advertised set from synopses rather than restating it.
- Inventory classifications: 108 entries, none unclassified. The installed registry is the denominator, and `deriveCliTransitionInventory` states that missing classifications remain leaves "so coverage cannot be made complete by omission" - while the registry under-reported the CLI surface, completeness was reachable by exactly that omission.
- `SchemaPolicyError` now carries the unresolved reference and appends it to the message at all three refusal sites, staying null where the refusal is not about a reference.
- `tests/adversarial/cli-diagnostic-classification.test.ts` no longer bounds each class body to 400 characters; a class that outgrows a window must not leave the denominator silently.

### Correction a successor must not repeat

`SCHEMA_REF_UNRESOLVED` from `transition inspect` is a **manifest-selection** question first. The default `graph/manifest.json` carries only the foundation source and cannot reach the graph-owned schema modules a descriptor references, so interrogating without `--manifest` refuses before producing any DAG. That refusal was read here as a corpus defect and "repaired" by importing the three module manifests into `graph/manifests/cli-transition-definition.json`. That import took each module identity from one binding subject to nine, nine and six, violated `schemaIdentity` uniqueness, and failed six tests in gate 15 that gate 14 had passed. Reverted at `eb63e44`. Never repair it that way; a manifest import composes every subject in that source into one corpus where identity uniqueness is an invariant.

### Exact next transitions

1. **USF-1189 delivery lane** is the critical path and the reason Hermes idles. `boundLaneCount: 0` against six unbound issue locators; `LINEAR_BINDING_WORK_INACTIVE` for `urn:usf:linear-binding:usf-1162-fixture-repair:2`. Use `usf linear-binding prepare|status|settle` and `usf hermes run --binding IRI`; never delete projections to force selection.
2. **USF-1192 contract depth.** 10 of the golden-path leaves expose the full descriptor contract (input derivation rules, produces/consumes, prerequisites, recovery, effect class); the rest expose help-level metadata only. Requirement 2, derivation transparency, is unmet for every non-adapter leaf. The issue also cannot close while `parallelSafety` is `unproven` and the end-to-end proof is gated on USF-1189.
3. **USF-1193** reproducibility lane not yet assessed against its acceptance.
4. **USF-1202** seed and clone cost. The seed fingerprint digests the **whole repository**, so any edit anywhere forces a ~17 min rebuild; cold is the common case in a develop-and-gate loop, and the cache is now ~168 MB across four fingerprints with no prune. Narrowing the fingerprint to the seed's real dependency set is worth more than the two clone-cost levers, and must be evidence-led: an unsound narrowing reuses a stale seed, which is far worse than a slow one.

Note: editing this file moves the seed fingerprint, so the next gate after this commit pays a cold rebuild. That is expected, not a fault.

Preserve the existing dirty ANQR work, live Hermes and independent candidate lanes. Earlier sections below are dated evidence and may describe superseded construction states.

## Repository hygiene — what is and is not disposable

`.usf/` is ignored and mostly disposable, but not uniformly, and the difference
costs real time if it is discovered by deleting the wrong half.

| path | disposable? |
|---|---|
| `.usf/workspaces/` | **yes**, once a check receipt exists. The receipt is the evidence; the directory is not. |
| `.usf/cache/` | **no.** This is the offline package mirror `usf init` resolves against. Removing it makes `usf init` fail with `ERR_PNPM_NO_OFFLINE_META` and no command that loads the graph will run. |
| `.usf/semantic-cache/`, `.usf/graph-executions/` | yes, at the cost of recomputation |
| `.usf/receipts/`, `.usf/proposals/` | keep: receipts are the evidence for work already done |

Measured on 2026-09-04: removing `.usf` entirely and running `usf init` fails
offline, so an empty-`.usf` clean clone is reproducible only with the mirror
supplied or with network access. That is the documented standalone-qualification
posture — the mirror is an expected input — but it is not the same claim as
"`.usf` is pure acceleration", and the two are easy to conflate.

## Naming

Terminology, namespaces, IRI construction, repository and path conventions are
defined once in [`naming.md`](naming.md). It is canonical: a second statement of
a rule is drift waiting to happen, so nothing here repeats it.

### A qualification no longer expires, and the CLI stopped hiding a command

Full record in
[`acceptance/2026-09-04-qualification-authority-and-cli-validity.md`](acceptance/2026-09-04-qualification-authority-and-cli-validity.md).

**The 900-second lifetime had no rationale.** `validitySeconds: 900` entered in
the same commit that set `probeFreshnessSeconds: 900`, and the decision record
justifies neither. They are different kinds of fact: whether a route answers now
decays, whether a model demonstrated competence does not. The routing replay had
decided it by identity all along — comparing corpus, policy and route digests and
never the clock — and only `observeQualifiedTaskClasses` disagreed. The newer
consumer now agrees with the established one, and the time parameter is gone, so
wall-clock expiry is unrepresentable rather than merely absent.

**The graders are why nothing qualifies, and eight of sixteen leak the answer.**
Run for real: `groq:openai/gpt-oss-120b` answered all four typescript cases and
passed one — the `json-schema` one. Eight cases require the model to reproduce
USF's own private vocabulary the prompt never supplies. One asked for the
TypeScript immutability modifier, got "The modifier is `readonly`", and scored
zero. The repair is designed in §4 of the record and **is the next step**; it
touches `config/qualification.json`, a different owner, and was not rushed into
this chain.

**`probe qualify` was implemented, reachable, and absent from help** — while the
command's own refusal message named it. `profile build`, `export` and `import`
were implemented, registered in the graph, and likewise absent. All four are in
help now, and a test asserts the dispatcher and help agree in both directions.

**Every provider adapter argv was validated against the installed CLI** — claude
2.1.251, codex-cli 0.152.0, agy 1.1.26 — flags and accepted values. All valid.
One doc defect fixed: the claude adapter's comment claimed a `--max-turns` flag
that its own inline comment three lines below said does not exist.

### `rdf-semantics` is outside Hermes, and no grader should be added for it

`modelCapability` on a work packet and `requiredCapability` on a dispatched unit
are different predicates. All nine `rdf-semantics` packets were realised through
the staged chain by an operator, not dispatched; Hermes has three prompt shapes
and none asks for N-Quads. Not stale, not a backlog, not a qualification gap.

### Provider routing reads authority now, and the qualification gate is live

Four defects were measured against the live surface and three were repaired;
the full evaluation and programme are in
[`acceptance/2026-09-04-provider-surface-evaluation.md`](acceptance/2026-09-04-provider-surface-evaluation.md).

**Catalogue price no longer authorizes anything.** 21 of 45 routable candidates
used to enter on `catalogue-declared-zero-price` with no declared authorization,
which is how OpenRouter came to carry 1 799 of 4 383 historical attempts while
no commit had ever authorized it. Authorization and spend permission are now two
axes: an authorization says which account may be used, `permittedCostClass` says
what for, and `paidSpendPermitted` says whether it may reach anything chargeable.
All eight accounts the operator holds are declared. 81 candidates, none on price.

**Antigravity was running in plan mode.** `agy --mode plan` answers a generation
request by writing a plan artefact and asking for approval, which is the whole
explanation for 20 of 20 invocations succeeding, 0 of 13 outputs accepted and
606 619 tokens spent. Pinned to `accept-edits`, verified against the binary from
a scratch working directory.

**Hermes never consulted qualification.** `graph execute` is the only path
`hermes implement` dispatches through and it passed no earned task classes, so a
qualification model holding thirteen adversarial properties constrained nothing.
It is passed now, and the input was made *required* — which immediately caught a
second caller, `usf probe availability`, with the same defect.

The gate binds nothing today and that is deliberate to state: both Hermes
entry points set `requiredCapabilities: []`, so no dispatched unit asks for a
task class. It binds when a supplied work graph declares one, or when
`modelCapability` is propagated into dispatched units — a separate decision.

**D4 was withdrawn rather than deferred.** Hermes has three prompt shapes and
none asks for N-Quads, so "put the strict profile in semantic-work prompts" had
no site. The real finding in its place: `rdf-semantics` is declared on nine work
packets, is unqualifiable because the suite grades no case for it, and is never
dispatched.

### Two lanes moved and both are back

Changing the economics evaluator, classification ladder, capability vocabulary
and route universe stales every work that reads them. Priced before the first
edit: three of four were already blocked and exactly one was verified-complete.
`provider-task-qualification` went blocked as predicted and was restored;
`provider-capability-universe` completed. Model ranking was **not** tuned —
routes earn position through the mechanism this programme connected.

### The foundation's architecture is declared and decidable

`graph/architecture-assurance.nq` could already state a topology and had never
stated this one: every instance in the corpus was `urn:anqr:`, and three of its
twenty types — enforcement binding, drift check and adversarial verification —
had never been instantiated by anyone. What was missing was not architecture
vocabulary but the machinery that makes a declared architecture decidable, so
`graph/architecture-conformance.nq` adds only that: declared roots and whether
each surface counts as running implementation, an enumerated population, eight
classifications each with the criterion and precedence that decide it, explicit
disposition decisions, assurance scope, a composable assurance set, and a
release claim with a declared absent-evidence disposition. Fifteen types,
thirty-two predicates, no new lifecycle and no parallel proof system.

Full report, including every disposition and all twelve planted negative cases:
[`acceptance/2026-09-04-architecture-assurance.md`](acceptance/2026-09-04-architecture-assurance.md).

### A wrong deletion was prevented by the denominator, not by review

Under a `src/`-only population, `src/providers/index.ts` was reached by nothing
— not the runtime, not the graph, not the tests — and the model reported it as
an unexplained orphan. Deleting it broke the typecheck: `recipes/` holds a
declared build-time recipe that imports it, and `recipes/` had never been in the
denominator. **The denominator was wrong, not the module.** The repair was to
declare the build-time root and widen the population; nothing was deleted.

This is the non-recursive-`readdir` defect one step further out. A denominator
that stops short of a *consumer* reports live implementation as abandoned, and
the population declaration now records why `recipes/` is in scope so nobody has
to rediscover it. If another root is ever added outside `src/` and `recipes/`,
it belongs in `populationRoot` in the same change.

### Two things the foundation genuinely does not have

The architecture is declared as measured, not as wished for. It is a module
architecture, not a hexagon: the realisation and provider layers both reach the
command surface, the semantic layer reaches the realisation layer, and fourteen
units sit in import cycles enumerated as a bounded exception. All of that is
declared with its reason, and tightening any of it is an architecture change
with its own work.

Six architecture-assurance types remain uninstantiated for the foundation.
`ArchitecturalPort` and `ArchitecturalAdapter` are absent because the foundation
is not ports-and-adapters. `DependencyScopeRule`, `FrameworkIsolationRule`,
`TestBoundaryRule` and `EnforcementDriftCheck` are real gaps, expressible today
and not claimed as covered.

### Two released digests no longer recompute, and nothing tracked changed

Core `e958768a` is still byte-identical. `anqr` and `anqr-with-foundation` are
not, and the cause was measured: neither manifest contains any source added in
this or the previous boundary, and the movement came entirely from
`.usf/conformance-evidence`, which is gitignored disposable state. Executing
conformance superseded the cached records the released recomputation had been
reading. The release record and tag are unmodified.

The check run at four consecutive boundaries was therefore reading disposable
local cache. That is the finding, not the digests.

### The blocker is a semantic contradiction, not missing work

All 16 remaining facets are architecture facets declaring
`implementation-conformance`, which means a subtest named for the facet that
**invokes the capability module**. An architecture property is not a behaviour
of a capability module, so the evaluator refuses such a subtest — correctly, by
the anti-self-warrant rule. Resolving it needs those facets to declare an
evidence class the architecture checker produces, and the evaluator to load it.

### The denominator is the deliverable

Reconciliation against the whole legacy application — 126 store actions, 185
API parameters, 33 payload serialisers, 12 routes, 5 functions, 86 locales, a
published OpenAPI document — found fourteen behaviours owned by no capability.
Revision 3 declares 58; revision 2 stays immutable at 44. An action denominator
beneath capabilities immediately earned itself by exposing two admin behaviours
that capability-level coverage could not show.

Two directive premises turned out to be wrong and are recorded as such: USF
already had 67 delivery contracts and 25 capability ports, so only nine were
genuinely absent; and the seven environments already existed, but as bare type
assertions with no posture, ordering or evidence requirements.

### Where it stops

Semantics, classification, enforcement and work derivation are complete and
checked. **No capability is implemented against revision 3**, the
integration-test environment is not instantiated, and staging and production
are unbound. Production-ready is not production-deployed, and neither is
claimed.

Using the current source module against the retained clean fixture, governed
completion publication applied successfully in 48.898s. The result was
`publicationState=created`, intent
`sha256:46f17a42ab4e2f5c68002ac1327bb6bf21197e1d427bc7f750554d7156dd9618`,
receipt digest
`sha256:aaf3cd09e17f4ef4090675538d05dae08dc4c02f3f34df50638e48e4ec4d8c45`,
and resulting semantic digest
`sha256:f3d7d8a7af29c6c96a648ae7722766e65bbede1b20eae34288e403bad3924482`.
Apply replay and verify both returned `matching` with the same receipt digest.
Native terminal evaluation then returned one accepted
`urn:usf:cli-contract:completion-publication-artifact:1` under evaluator
digest `sha256:b9c8fd3a8e648ecb1ddb6e1bbdca0d4e536ebfeffdfd8f204a38287370b0ea3d`.
This is isolated-fixture publication and replay evidence only; full-gate,
primary-repository integration, Linear readback, and USF-1192 completion
remain open.

Retained-fixture terminal interrogation is explicit: the native evaluator
returned no authenticated artifacts; ordinary completion is `missing` with
`TERMINAL_BRANCH_ARTIFACT_MISSING` and `TERMINAL_BRANCH_PROOF_MISSING`, while
ordinary construction is `unknown` with `TERMINAL_BRANCH_INPUT_MISSING`.
The evaluator rule itself authenticated, so this is a genuine missing
publication/terminal artifact boundary rather than a check-seed refusal.

The latest clean fixture retained all five pass receipts after the 1200-second
producer observation timed out. A bounded replay of only the retained current
state returned `current` in 298.361s and persisted a complete five-member seed
with `receiptSetDigest=sha256:2778a628e54970e566b6771f3368a9eedd78ba09b248989b5fe2ad97cf518d5e`.
No validation launch occurred during that replay and no process remains. This
proves durable parent settlement from retained receipts, but not that the
original clean integration test completed within its outer bound; zero-launch
proof/lineage consumer evidence and a clean accepted producer run remain open.

The clean producer was retried once after the parent reconciliation and work /
runtime binding repairs. It reached the 1200-second outer bound and exited
124 with no accepted seed, publication, or effect. The retained fixture
replay remains the strongest positive evidence: `prepareCompletionChecks`
returned `current` in 295.169s and persisted the authenticated five-member
seed above. No child process remains. Do not repeat the clean producer without
first reducing or instrumenting the end-to-end runtime/settlement path; do not
widen the semantic check bound or treat retained-fixture state as clean-clone
evidence.

### The first experience is a chain, not a screen

`semantic capability → experience intent → Future Use Pattern → product
experience profile → design semantics → interaction/state contract →
implementation binding → Preact realisation → rendered evidence → adversarial
validation`. Preact sits at the implementation end and holds no semantic
authority: the state machine, the permitted transitions, the design roles and
the admissible parameter join are all framework-free and all projected from the
graph. Replacing Preact would not touch any of them.

### Profiles are documented

[`profiles.md`](profiles.md) records the three distinct things called a profile
— core realisation profiles, product realisation profiles and product profile
revisions — with each one's exact contract selection, why revisions exist
instead of edits, and the observed lane digests. It is a projection of the
graph, not authority.

### The suite was order-dependent and is not any more

Two test files planted violations into the shared working tree. Node runs each
test file as its own process, so the plants interleaved with the sibling file's
known-good assertion: the same suite passed twice and failed once, and the
failing test moved. A fresh clone showing 288/289 against the primary tree's
289/289 is what exposed it. Planting now happens in a disposable copy of the
real tree, with no assertion weakened. Ten consecutive clean runs in the
primary tree, three in the clone.

### The output boundary is real

Five declared-but-missing contracts are implemented against their ports, with
their real capability limits preserved rather than smoothed into false
symmetry: `svg-container` (encode, one frame, no rasteriser, row runs merged),
`png-container` (real chunks, CRC32, fixed deflate level for reproducible
bytes), `gif-container` (the only animated container; refuses a palette beyond
256 colours rather than quantising), `system-clock`, and `png-image-source` (a
real PNG decoder reversing all five filters; PNG only, because a lossy format
needs a DCT and is refused instead of approximated).

End-to-end proof: a real QR symbol is rasterised, encoded to PNG, SVG and GIF,
decoded back through the image source, resampled to a module grid, and decoded
by the codec to the exact payload. No mocked codec, no mocked container.

### The enforcement was worthless and is not any more

Probed before implementing: the old check asked whether *any* file existed
beneath the adapters component, so one file satisfied all six adapters, deleting
an adapter went undetected, and an empty file passed. Now each adapter resolves
to its own derived path, must be owned by a component in its declared layer,
must import the contract of every port it claims, and must bind an **exported**
annotated value — which proves the type checker is engaged, and tsc then decides
conformance. Twelve planted proofs cover missing port, missing adapter with a
sibling present, empty file, marker export, annotated-but-not-exported, port
bypass, wrong component, novelty, unknown port claim, and a compiler-backed case
where an incompatible adapter fails `tsc`.

### Drift enforcement is registered

`scripts/derive-anqr-architecture-rules.mjs` projects graph to product rules;
`tests/contract/anqr-architecture-rules-drift.test.ts` is reached by `npm test`
— placed in `tests/contract` deliberately, because `test:full` does not glob
`tests/conformance` and putting it there would have been another enumeration
blind spot. It fails on output drift and on authority drift, both planted, and
the graph is what wins. ANQR additionally refuses an incoherent projection
before computing any verdict.

### Next work item

**Repair the qualification graders.** The freshness half of D5 is done; this is
the half that still blocks everything. Eleven of sixteen graders are brittle and
eight leak USF's private vocabulary as the expected answer, so no model can
qualify for reasons unrelated to competence. The replacement is designed
case-by-case in §4 of the record, needs no new grader kind, and must keep grading
pure because `routeWork` replays it. Owner: `provider-semantic-qualification`.

Then run the suite across the authorized population, and only then decide whether
to propagate `modelCapability` into dispatched `requiredCapability` — turning that
on today would stop dispatch entirely, since nothing is qualified.

Also open: D6 (census verdicts are unconditional literals), the `usf graph`
refusal message that omits `lanes`, and the `RegisteredCliCommand` gap (27 of
about 74 surface points declared).

Also open, unchanged: the ANQR architecture evidence-class contradiction (§6 of the acceptance record),
which is graph authority plus a USF source change through their own staged
chains. Then the testing rung, localisation, browser evidence and per-action
proof — all listed with what each needs in
[`acceptance/2026-09-04-anqr-testing-realisation.md`](acceptance/2026-09-04-anqr-testing-realisation.md).

### Open and honestly unfinished

- **Eight facets remain undecidable and were not turned green.** Two of them
  could have been argued and were not: `surfaceparity` has one surface where
  parity needs two comparable subjects, and `localeparity` has no locale
  resource — strings existing is not parity. The other six await evidence
  classes this lane does not produce.
- The architecture checker is a regex scanner, not a TypeScript AST parse. It
  over-reports and never under-reports; conformance is the compiler's call.
- ANQR's rules-integrity check cannot catch a coordinated edit that stays
  internally consistent; only the USF-side authority comparison can.
- No continuous integration exists in the product repository, so the
  order-dependence above was found by hand rather than by a second runner.
- One rendered surface exists. Android, iPhone, API and MCP realisations are
  modelled and unrealised.
- **Priority 1 publication debt stands unchanged.** The packet still reports
  `COMPLETION_CONSTRUCTION_AUTHORITY_NOT_CURRENT` and
  `COMPLETION_POSTIMAGE_VERIFIER_PROOFBLOCKED:work-integration-transition:1`.
  All three declared versions of that Foundation verifier are proofblocked, so
  the byte-delta completion route cannot be published without implementing it
  or falsely claiming no-change. Neither was done, and neither was worked
  around.
- Nothing is pushed.

## Architecture evidence-class rebinding (2026-09-04)

USF `1ae1373`, clean. ANQR `585f25057`, clean. Hermes live on
`graph/manifests/anqr-application.json`, authority
`sha256:ba5913a41d71206f5be4c034253507944c042310e581a351018a00296bd775c3`.

**What was wrong.** Fifteen ANQR architecture requirements declared
`admissibleEvidenceClass "implementation-conformance"` while declaring a corpus
`requirementScope`. The class admits only a subtest named for the requirement
that invokes one capability module, and no export of one module knows what the
others import, so the artefact Hermes was asked for could not be written
truthfully. Eight capabilities spent five repair rounds each on it. The control
case proves the mechanism was never the problem: `architecturesurfaceparity` is
the one architecture concern expressible as a call on a module's own exports,
and BAU generated and satisfied it in a single cycle.

**What changed.** Corpus requirements are now observed at corpus scope through a
declared `CorpusObservationProcedure`. Nothing was invented: `structural-observation`
is the class the foundation's own architecture requirements already declare,
`assurance-scope:corpus`, `conformanceProcedure` and `requirementClass "structural"`
already existed, and ANQR already had a checker returning a per-facet verdict
over the real tree. Only the path from that evidence to the evaluator was
missing. Observations execute through the same confinement as every other check
and are recorded bound to a digest of exactly the corpus they observed.

**Result.** Architecture facets `BLOCKED 15 → 4`, `SATISFIED 1 → 12`, with
`UNSATISFIED`, `STALE` and `NOT_ASSESSED` all 0. `repairExhausted 8 → 0` on
every manifest. All four manifests are at `ready 0`.

**The four that remain BLOCKED are correct, not a backlog.** Static type
correctness, build correctness and deterministic artefact production each need
a child process, and an observation runs inside a confinement that grants none,
so the declared procedure records them undecided rather than passed. Deciding
them needs an observation vehicle that may spawn the toolchain — a real
follow-up, and not something to fix by loosening the confinement. Locale parity
is required and unrealised; no procedure should assert it until it is built.

**Separate reporting defect, not causal here.** `src/commands/hermes-implement.ts:697`
sets `dispatchedUnits: pending.length` while actual dispatch is gated on
`envFile !== null` near line 490, so a credential-free run reports units
dispatched having contacted no provider. Same class as the D6 census literals.
Unowned and unrepaired.


## The four remaining architecture requirements (2026-09-04)

USF `eae26c0`, clean. ANQR `915e03a14`, clean. Hermes live on
`anqr-application`. Architecture facets `SATISFIED 14`, `BLOCKED 2`,
`UNSATISFIED 0`, `STALE 0`. All four manifests `ready 0`, `repairExhausted 0`.

They were not four of a kind:

- **Deterministic artefact production — wrong assumption, now decided.** It was
  grouped with the subprocess requirements on the belief that determinism meant
  repeating the build. Artefact production is a library call with an injectable
  clock, so the corpus procedure produces one specification twice and compares
  bytes.
- **Static type correctness — real, and the authority already existed.** Every
  conformance run already shells to USF's own locked `tsc` outside the
  confinement, on the stated ground that it reads sources and never executes
  them. That exemption is now declared: a `CorpusToolObservationProcedure` names
  a host tool carrying `tool-execution-class:source-reader`.
- **Build correctness — real, and deliberately still blocked.** The declared
  build executes the corpus, so it does not carry the reader class. Deciding it
  needs an execution authority for a tool that runs product code. That is an
  operator decision, not an inference from the requirement being blocking.
- **Locale parity — real, unrealised, correctly blocked.** No locale resource
  exists in the product repository. The 86 resources at 1676 keys each are
  legacy evidence and the graph already records bringing them across as an
  unresolved delivery decision with a known denominator. Hermes must not derive
  work for it: a generated string table would evidence nothing.

**The reader-tool authority, and its limits.** Tool must be a declared
`HostToolDependency` carrying the source-reader class; operation selects a fixed
argument template held in implementation, so no argument comes from the graph;
executable path must be repository-relative inside USF's locked dependencies;
environment carries no PATH; time and output bounded; absent tool records a
refusal. It is **not** network-sandboxed the way the bwrap vehicle is — the
grant rests on the tool being a reader, not on isolation. Every observation
record now names the authority it ran under, and one naming neither is
inadmissible.

**Do not put a declaration a product profile needs into `graph/semantic-first.nq`.**
It is currentness-bound: nine quads there failed fifteen transition and
publication tests with `TRANSITION_CURRENT_POSTSTATE_MISMATCH`. Foundation
vocabulary a product imports belongs in `graph/architecture-assurance.nq`.


## Grader semantics (2026-09-05)

USF `5b86ab5`, clean. ANQR `915e03a14`, clean and untouched — this lane is
foundation-only. Architecture facets unchanged at `SATISFIED 14`, `BLOCKED 2`.
Hermes live throughout, cycle 188, blockers 0.

**What a grader is, now stated.** Each kind declares what it asserts, its
judgement mode — comparison, selection, structural-validation or
marker-detection, and deliberately never *proof* — and the dimensions it is
competent to judge. A grade is one of PASS, FAIL, REFUSAL, INAPPLICABLE or
INSUFFICIENT_EVIDENCE with a machine-readable diagnostic. Only PASS scores, and
an unjudgeable case stays in the denominator: dropping it would raise the score
of a suite that failed to ask the question.

**The audit is mechanical and reproduced the manual one exactly** — eleven of
sixteen cases defective: four whose grader could not judge the dimension, one
whose options no prompt supplied, and eight requiring vocabulary private to this
repository. Eight cases repaired to be answerable from what they state; thirteen
of sixteen are now clean with **zero hard defects**. The remaining three require
recall of general engineering knowledge, which is legitimate and is reported
rather than hidden.

**Anti-circularity is structural, not asserted.** Grading is a pure total
function of case and answer, proven against the source so no later edit can
reach a clock, a filesystem or a provider. No grader can ask a model, so model
agreement can never become evidence. One answer evidences one case: reuse across
cases now evidences nothing, so a single generic response cannot be multiplied
by several graders happening to accept it.

**Hermes did not exercise the grader path, and cannot.** All three dispatch
sites set `requiredCapabilities: []`, so ordinary BAU requests no task class —
verified: the newest qualification record predates the running supervisor by
hours. The gate is connected and binds nothing, which remains the intended
state. It binds when a supplied work graph declares `requiredCapability`, or if
`modelCapability` is ever propagated into dispatched units — still an unmade
decision, and not one to make by manufacturing work to demonstrate a mechanism.

**The suite moved to 1.1.0**, which supersedes stored qualification by design: a
changed case changes what the task class means. Nothing was lost — the two
stored records carry no corpus binding at all and were already inadmissible.

**Editing `config/qualification.json` refuses `usf route` until it is committed**
(`ROUTE_QUALIFICATION_AUTHORITY_UNCURRENT`): the authority is compared against
the Git-indexed blob, not the working tree.

## 2026-09-09 failed-check diagnostic retention and validation boundary

The confined graph-derived check kernel now retains bounded stdout/stderr
observations for a failed, timed-out, or output-limited child when a native
STARTED intent exists. It writes a no-replace diagnostic record below
`.usf/diagnostics/check/<candidate>/<validation>/`, bound to the existing
intent, execution-input, candidate, validation and execution-authority
digests. It does not mint a successful receipt or alter the UNKNOWN refusal
rule. Existing receipt/intent identities remain the source of truth.

## 2026-09-09 validation decomposition blocker

The graph-selected `urn:usf:validation:cli-transition-definition-adapter`
command currently bundles three fast contract/parser files with
`tests/unit/commands/transition.test.ts`. The three fast files completed 73/73
tests in 18.9 seconds. The transition file alone was interrupted at 390.89
seconds after only its first reported test; the first native inspection took
58.1 seconds. The five-minute graph bound is therefore not a viable bound for
that bundled command.

Do not raise this bound above five minutes. The next acceptance-bearing change
must decompose or replace the graph validation obligation into bounded focused
cohorts while preserving the semantic coverage it represents. Until then the
check-receipt diagnostic slice remains unaccepted and no full gate should be
spent on the unchanged candidate.

The isolated `--test-name-pattern=installed` transition subset was also run
once. It took 419.74 seconds and failed at
`tests/unit/commands/transition.test.ts:363`: authenticated native support did
not remove any `CLI_OPERATIONAL_RULE_UNRESOLVED` diagnostics. This is a
semantic operational-support projection failure, not only a duration failure;
repair the projection before further expensive validation retries.

## 2026-09-12 parallel assessment evaluator checkpoint

The parallel-safety verdict is no longer a hard-coded literal. `inspectCliTransition`
previously emitted `parallelSafety: { state: "unproven", ... }` inline, so no
assessment could ever be read and no wrong assessment could ever be refused.
`deriveCliParallelSafety` in `src/graph/cli-transitions.ts` now derives the
verdict from admitted `urn:usf:type:CliParallelSafetyAssessment` records bound
to the selected contract, and `authenticateCliParallelSafetyAssessment` is
exported beside it as the pure semantic authentication of a proved-shaped
record.

Authentication refuses, by exact reason code: a stale outer or basis semantic
digest; an assessment rule other than the graph-owned boundary; a member set
that is not the current cohort; a required pair count unreachable for that
cohort size; an assessed count disagreeing with the pairs presented; self,
duplicate and reversed pairs; pairs naming a transition outside the cohort;
incomplete pair coverage in either direction; a `compatible` pair whose write
scope meets the other side's read or write scope by directory prefix; a
`compatible` pair sharing a non-file resource; conflict entries or a `conflict`
outcome inside a proved assessment; scope and resource digests not recomputed
from the pairs actually presented; a verifier that is a cohort member, the
contract or the assessment itself; absent verification evidence; and an
obligation set omitting the boundary rule. A scope that cannot be stated
canonically — absolute, traversing, scheme-prefixed or empty — is refused
rather than read as disjoint.

`tests/unit/graph/parallel-safety.test.ts` covers this with 27 cases and passed
27/27 in 0.126s, including the counterexamples the boundary requires: impossible
denominator, duplicate, self and reversed pairs, an empty pair list over a
multi-member cohort, and a bare proved tag. Two positive cases confirm a
complete two-member and three-member assessment authenticates, and one confirms
sibling paths sharing only a name prefix are not an overlap.

The selected frontier is unchanged. `transition inspect --root
urn:usf:work:cli-transition-definition` still returns exit 1, 25 diagnostics,
`complete=false`, `executionAuthority=none`, `parallelSafety=unproven` and body
digest `sha256:059788a900655d9e4839f339c374bd48760f4e1764ea5439fe20139e223844d6`,
byte-identical to the digest recorded in the preceding checkpoint. No
assessment record is admitted in the corpus, so the ordinary path still returns
unproven with its exact missing obligation; the difference is that the state is
now derived rather than declared.

Graph source was not changed. `rule-parallel-assessment-boundary:1` requires
both an evaluator and admitted typed assessment records before a verdict may
leave unproven; only the evaluator now exists, so the rule text remains
accurate and no transaction was needed. Producing a real assessment record —
which requires per-transition read/write scope and non-file resource data — is
the next increment, and is what would move `parallelSafety` off unproven.

TypeScript no-emit and `git diff --check` pass. The contract/transition/parser/
feedback cohort passed 126/126 in 251.669s on these bytes, and the graph unit
cohort passed 88/88 in 8.785s. No full gate was run, nothing
was committed or pushed, stewardship was not claimed, Hermes was neither
restarted nor stopped, and no provider was contacted. No execution authority,
parallel-safety proof, terminal satisfaction, USF-1192 acceptance or USF-1189
cutover is claimed by this checkpoint.

## 2026-09-12 scope-derivation evidence class and retained gate

### Retained full gate

`node scripts/test-full.mjs --all` ran to completion and retained its result at
`.usf/logs/full-gate/20260912T043207450Z-3582099.json`, result digest
`sha256:02a05fed6fabb98bbc60676dba839aeb214b642e3f6b7df0170fb877f5b77008`,
log digest `sha256:ab205d3cf191582eae9bacd6423b8d798668608577c0986c60296c38b5c2480c`.
Status `failed`, exit 1, reason `ORDINARY_TESTS_FAILED`, elapsed 4099114 ms
(68.3 minutes), selecting `urn:usf:work:fixture-process-runtime` at basis
`sha256:b884126862e4fb5ee0a4f71fc3e83f68121d7d9036a8c8cd692074483effa5c7`.
1072 assertions passed and exactly one test failed:
`concurrent real starts have one winner, and an unreachable configured GitHub
remote is not contacted`, with `lifecycle exited before expected event (8, null)`.

Exit 8 is `CONTINUOUS_DEGRADED`. Run focused, that file passes 3/3 in 27.2 s and
the same case takes 7203 ms against 1475 ms under the gate: the supervisor is
CPU-starved by 117 concurrent siblings and exits degraded before its event. The
failure is contention, not lifecycle behaviour, and it touches no file changed
in this session. `scripts/test-full.mjs` now runs real-process lifecycle tests
in a serialized `serial-tests` phase at concurrency 1 instead of starving them
at concurrency 4. Coverage is unchanged: the plan reports 117 ordinary tests,
`serialTests: ["tests/integration/lifecycle-process.test.ts"]`,
`serialConcurrency: 1`, and the file is still executed, not skipped.

### The scope-derivation evidence class

Every effect contract in the selected manifest declares a `scopeDerivationRule`;
`scopeDerivationRules` asserts this against the real corpus rather than a
fixture. Static `readScope`/`writeScope` quads exist for only some effects and
are a partial illustration, which `local-preparation-scope:1` states outright:
"Empty static scope arrays do not assert that context-derived scopes are empty;
operational scope derivation remains mandatory." Four transitions declare prose
such as "Exact selected native candidate basis, schema and retained artifact
dependencies"; five of the remaining six declare no write scope at all, including
`completion-publication` and `no-change-workspace`, which plainly write. An
absent array therefore means derived, never empty, and an empty scope overlaps
nothing -- the same false compatibility the evaluator exists to refuse, one level
below the conflict list.

`src/graph/transition-scope-derivation.ts` is the missing evidence class. It
projects concrete read/write sets and file preimages from the runtime basis that
produced them, binds them to the basis, candidate, schema, dependency and
preimage digests, and carries an independent verifier and evidence identities.
`authenticateScopeDerivations` refuses, by exact reason code: a derivation
missing for a member whose rule demands one, ambiguous or duplicated, naming a
rule the member does not declare, stale against the current basis, incomplete,
unauthenticated, self-verified, non-canonical or prose, empty in both directions,
or carrying a tampered digest. `deriveCliParallelSafety` now accepts that
evidence and, when every rule-derived member is authenticated, cross-checks each
pair's declared scopes and resources against what was actually observed and
re-derives each outcome, refusing `TRANSITION_PARALLEL_SAFETY_SCOPE_NOT_DERIVED`
and `TRANSITION_PARALLEL_SAFETY_OUTCOME_CONTRADICTED`. Without that evidence the
cohort stays blocked on the rule, never on an authored path list. The module
declares its own basis type structurally so the graph layer does not import the
realisation layer, which imports it back.

### Verification

`tests/unit/graph/scope-derivation.test.ts` passes 15/15 and
`tests/unit/graph/parallel-safety.test.ts` passes 32/32; the graph unit cohort
passes 108/108 in 8.73 s. TypeScript no-emit and `git diff --check` pass. The
selected frontier is unchanged at body digest
`sha256:059788a900655d9e4839f339c374bd48760f4e1764ea5439fe20139e223844d6`
with 25 diagnostics, `complete=false`, `executionAuthority=none` and
`parallelSafety=unproven`, which is correct: no assessment record is admitted
and no construction authority is published, so nothing may leave unproven.

### Construction authority is earned, not granted

`usf work` reports permission `urn:usf:contract-state:proofblocked` for
`urn:usf:work:cli-transition-definition`, with `BOOTSTRAP_EXCEPTION_NOT_DECLARED`,
`CONSTRUCTION_EVIDENCE_MISSING`, `CONSTRUCTION_PROOF_RESULT_MISSING` and
`REALISATION_PERMISSION_NOT_ACTIVE`. The six refusing adapters therefore refuse
correctly. Fresh `usf evidence construction` and `usf prove construction` on the
current basis `sha256:33816a48ff57b67ac00...` return `outcome: "pass"` with
`reasonCodes: []` and `applicability: "required"`, and
`usf publish construction-authority --verify` returns `status: "ok"`. The
obligation is genuinely satisfied by the existing implementation; it was
proofblocked only because no evidence had been published for the current basis.
`--apply` was not run: the session's permission classifier denied both the
publication and staging its bundle under `.usf/candidates/`. No authority was
granted, no diagnostic was suppressed, and the 25 diagnostics stand.

## 2026-09-12 confined-closure regression and its repair

The first acceptance run after the scope-derivation module failed seven tests.
Three were host contention: the run coincided with a system memory sweep that
killed background processes, and `an authorised real fixture supervisor starts`,
`help is repository-free` and the two lifecycle cases pass focused in a quiet
environment (3102 ms against 17110 ms under the gate). That run is an
interrupted observation, not an authenticated result; it aborted after
1034438 ms without reaching the serial phase.

Four were a genuine regression this session introduced, and the diagnosis is
worth keeping. `src/graph/cli-transitions.ts` appears in the `repositoryReadFile`
closure of three confined validations:
`urn:usf:validation-runtime-closure:cli-transition-definition:1`,
`urn:usf:validation-runtime-closure:local-contribution:1` and
`urn:usf:validation-runtime-closure:realisation-basis-chain-focused:1`.
Adding a runtime import of `./transition-scope-derivation.ts` to that file made
the module unresolvable inside every confined child, which surfaced as
`BOOTSTRAP_CHECK_REFUSED` / `CHECK_FAILED` with empty stderr in
`tests/integration/bootstrap-loop.test.ts` and as three failures in
`tests/adversarial/realisation-check.test.ts`.

The repair was to invert the dependency rather than widen the closure. The
import is now type-only and erased at compile time, `DerivedScopeEvidence`
carries derivations the caller has already authenticated, and the pairwise
outcome is recomputed with the overlap comparison `cli-transitions.ts` already
owns, now tolerant of the trailing slash a basis path may carry. No runtime
closure, confinement boundary or graph source was changed. A file inside a
confined closure must not gain runtime imports; that is the reusable rule.

`tests/adversarial/realisation-check.test.ts` now passes 9/9,
`tests/integration/bootstrap-loop.test.ts` 2/2, and the graph unit cohort
108/108. A test file must also not read the corpus at module scope:
`scope-derivation.test.ts` now loads the production graph lazily on first use,
because a test file is imported in execution views that do not carry it.

## 2026-09-12 locale preparation for the full claimed denominator

Legacy translations stay legacy. `bak/anqr/tools/i18n` was not read, copied or
adopted, and no translated value was generated anywhere: the graph is explicit
that "Generating translations to satisfy a count would be worse than the gap."
What was missing was the other half of the disposition -- "A parity check must
compare each locale against the reference locale by key set, not by file
presence" -- and the resources for a translator to work in.

`scripts/derive-anqr-locales.mjs` projects the claimed language denominator out
of `graph/products/anqr/localisation-model.nq` into
`../anqr/packages/architecture/locales.json`, the same graph-is-authority
pattern the architecture and experience projections use. It derives 86 claimed
locales, reference `en-GB`, and 7 right-to-left locales (`ar`, `fa`, `fa-AE`,
`fa-AF`, `fa-IR`, `iw-IL`, `ur`), refusing an unresolved claim, an ambiguous
reference or an incomplete locale. `tests/contract/anqr-architecture-rules-drift.test.ts`
now fails in both directions for the registry as well, and additionally asserts
the denominator itself, because a registry that quietly lost locales would still
round-trip. That cohort passes 10/10.

In the product, `src/capabilities/localisation/localeparity.ts` decides parity by
key set alone and `packages/architecture/tools/prepare-locales.mjs` prepares
every claimed locale. The reference `en-GB` resource carries the 21 interface
strings the product's own surfaces actually use, extracted from `apps/web/src`
rather than invented. All 85 other locales are prepared with that exact key set
and no value against any key, so a translator has somewhere to work and the gap
stays visible. Existing translations are preserved and retired keys dropped;
preparation is idempotent, and `locales:check` is now part of `npm run validate`.

The assessment reports `reference`, `complete`, `partial`, `prepared` or
`absent` per locale, and refuses a resource for an unclaimed locale, a duplicate,
or a direction disagreeing with the graph. An empty string is not a translation.
Today it reports 86 claimed, 1 reference, 85 prepared, 0 complete -- the honest
state, and the reason no procedure is bound to the `localeparity` architecture
facet: preparation is not translation, and binding a procedure now would let key
presence read as coverage. The facet stays undecided, which is what the graph
asks for.

ANQR `npm run validate` exits 0: 418 tests pass, architecture reports
`unaccounted 0, blocking violations 0, conforming true`, the web build produces
48494 bytes from 27 inputs, and the delivery check reports
`blocking 0, conforming true`. The preparation tool lives under
`packages/architecture/`, inside the existing `assurance-and-test-tooling`
module boundary, so no new boundary was declared and no architecture rule was
widened.

### Stewardship ownership is bound to the immediate parent

Three publication attempts failed `STEWARD_CLAIM_STALE` before succeeding, and
the cause is worth recording. The CLI binds a claim to the invoking process's
**immediate parent**. Running `steward recover` inside `$(...)` binds it to the
command-substitution subshell; wrapping it in `timeout` binds it to the
`timeout` process. Both exit immediately, so the claim is dead before the
guarded operation runs, and the observed bound pid differs from the shell's own
`$$` by a few. The working form is: export the session id, run `recover` as a
direct child of the durable shell with its output redirected to a file rather
than captured through a subshell, read the claim and generation from that file,
then run the guarded operation and the release in the same shell with no
wrapper of any kind. Never wrap a guarded operation in `timeout`.

## 2026-09-12 clean-clone fixture repaired after first current-basis publication

The first acceptance run after publication failed one test:
`installed transition inspect reduces native operational gaps on a genuinely
authorised root without effects`, with `CONSTRUCTION_PUBLICATION_PARTIAL_STATE`
(exit 7) raised by the fixture's own publication step, not by the inspection
under test.

The fixture builds a clean pre-publication checkout and stripped retained
construction grants from `graph/cli-transition-definition-grants.nq`. A
publication emits three record kinds together -- the grant, the ProofResult that
authorised it, and the Evidence that produced it -- and only the grant was being
removed. That looked sufficient for as long as every retained Evidence and
ProofResult had been derived against an older basis and therefore could not
satisfy the current one. The first publication on the current basis ended that:
the fixture kept current-basis Evidence and ProofResult with no grant, which is
precisely the partial state the publisher is built to refuse.

The strip now covers all three subjects. The fixture passes 1/1 in 271.2 s and
reports 25 diagnostics before construction and 0 after, with
`executionAuthority` none and `parallelSafety` unproven throughout. No
confinement, runtime closure, assertion or publisher behaviour was weakened; the
fixture's own notion of "clean pre-publication" was incomplete and is now
complete. A fixture that strips publication output must strip the whole stage.

## 2026-09-12 stewardship exclusivity joins the serialized phase

The acceptance run on the published basis failed one test:
`an authorised real fixture supervisor starts, survives handoff, drains and
restarts under its steward`, refused `STEWARD_ACTIVITY_BUSY` with exit 8 after
1314 ms. Run alone the file passes 9/9 and that case takes 3191 ms. It had
passed in the preceding clean gate, so the collision is intermittent rather
than ordered.

The cause is the same class as the lifecycle failure repaired earlier. The test
asserts that no other steward operation is active, which concurrency inherently
violates: a sibling test touching stewardship at the wrong moment makes it
refuse. `tests/integration/hermes-stewardship.test.ts` therefore joins
`tests/integration/lifecycle-process.test.ts` in the serialized phase. Coverage
is identical; only scheduling changes, and nothing was relaxed to make a
refusal pass. The plan now reports 117 ordinary tests and two serial tests.

Retained failing run: `.usf/logs/full-gate/20260912T101043270Z-54323.json`,
result digest `sha256:314c3778f7e8d247f51e7ca9f1709b72e8e9aa41b72cef7afcf3de3df1e31209`,
1085 assertions passed, one failed, elapsed 4255454 ms.

### Authority must be published last

Immediately after that gate the frontier read 25 diagnostics again, with the
grant and its receipt still present. This is correct behaviour, not a
regression. A grant binds an exact RealisationBasis, and the source edits made
*after* the first publication -- the clean-clone fixture repair, the serial-phase
extension and the handover entries -- moved the basis from
`sha256:40ccc4235674aeb31004aba...` to
`sha256:ca159c0d4b16d729b6c02bdb1ac14f9601365f06eeac320bc06f5db6b5824497`, so the
retained evidence and proof no longer satisfied it and the permission returned
to `proofblocked`. Publish construction authority *after* the last source change,
never before.

Construction authority was rederived and republished against the gated bytes.
`prove` returned `outcome: "pass"`, `reasonCodes: []` at basis
`sha256:ca159c0d4b16d729b6c02bdb1ac14f9601365f06eeac320bc06f5db6b5824497`;

base `sha256:945048534457d3daf9f5837e615bf13a9b061b22955bc40a7c264711a0fafdf6`
to resulting `sha256:03c6032481aaa74bf2ddeac318c7732d57d4cda96c426318995c4b3e5580464f`.

## 2026-09-13 programme convergence tranche

Gate on commit 9a3748c passed and is retained at
`.usf/logs/full-gate/20260912T225307104Z-1595570.json`, result digest
`sha256:2ada633ed418eb41a512660640d2a180fe2e1e45f3235d1996f538411299b068`,
log digest `sha256:51255d884c6cf0d5ec9647e0db6f4c5bafcc0e73099116305fd442dfc1e8d03a`.
Status `passed`, exit 0, phase `complete`, 3988694 ms, 1091 assertions, zero
failures, all three phases.

USF-1192 names one acceptance that nothing measured: "the parallel plan and the
sequential semantic transition interpreter converge on the same terminal
required artifact set for the same basis. Any discrepancy is a correctness
failure." `tests/unit/graph/transition-convergence.test.ts` measures it, 5/5:
the wave projection covers the required transition denominator exactly once and
agrees with `requiredTransitions`; every wave member's consumed artifacts are
produced strictly earlier, with artifacts nothing in the cohort produces treated
as external inputs that constrain no ordering; the terminal set derived by
walking the waves equals the interpreter's own `terminalClosure.derived`; and the
derivation is deterministic for one basis, including its interrogation digest.

A fourth case exists to stop this convergence being misread. Agreement about
artifacts is not a statement about concurrent execution, so the same test asserts
`topologicalOnly`, `parallelSafety.state = unproven`, `executionAuthority = none`
and that the unproven assessment still names its missing obligation. Wave
membership is a topological fact and is denied as evidence of anything else.

`parallelSafety` stays `unproven` deliberately. USF-1192 requires parallelisation
to be *derived* from semantic dependencies, read/write overlap, integration
ownership, provider limits and concurrency policy, and forbids inferring it from
file names or apparent independence. It does not require this cohort to be
proved, and the evaluator would refuse a proof it does not have: every effect
declares a `scopeDerivationRule`, so no authored path list can establish it.

## 2026-09-13 USF-1189 blocker and the check diagnostic repair

The autonomous Linear implementation is present, not missing: `src/commands/linear-client.ts`
is a real GraphQL client, `src/commands/hermes-linear.ts` drives it under
`urn:usf:policy:hermes-linear-transport:1`, the CLI exposes
`hermes start|status|stop --linear-policy`, and `config/hermes-linear.json`
carries endpoint, workspaces, teams, credential name and bounds. Its declared
validation set passes 33/33 run directly.

Construction authority for `urn:usf:work:hermes-linear-autonomy` was earned and
published (receipt
`evidence/construction-authority/b3eda33e6495d12621b11109a65c88ab5ead50a731af2d0f7de88870d4afde44.json`),
taking the work to construction eligible, active-permission, maximumThrough
integrate. Runtime remains correctly refused: `readLinearPolicySource(runtime)`
requires `completion.verified`, which is false pending the no-change receipt.

The blocker was that the graph-derived check refused `CHECK_FAILED` carrying an
exit code and nothing else. `readGraphDerivedCheckFailureDiagnostic` returned
`absent` and `.usf/diagnostics/check/` did not exist, because
`persistFailureDiagnostic` is gated on a started execution intent. A failure
observed without one was unreadable, and indistinguishable from every other
`CHECK_FAILED`.

`graphDerivedCheck` now carries the bounded observation itself — exit code,
timeout and signal, output-limit and truncation state, observed byte counts and
the captured stdout/stderr — on every failing return where no intent-bound
diagnostic can be persisted, marked `executionIntentStarted: false`. It invents
no intent it does not have and changes no confinement, receipt or authority
semantics. `tests/adversarial/realisation-check.test.ts` and
`tests/unit/realisation/workspace.test.ts` pass 25/25 together.

That repair moved the basis, which correctly staled the construction authority
published minutes earlier and made the candidate refuse `STALE_REALISATION_BASIS`.
This is the ordering rule enforcing itself: finish source, then gate, then
publish. Authority for both works is republished after the final gate.
